# Test-Coverage Manifest — Part 3

> **Status: PROPOSED — inventory, not a decision record.** Every row carries a `PROPOSED:`
> prefix. Deletion authority is human; no keep/retire call in this file is settled until reviewed.
>
> **Re-pointed 2026-08-28 by task 3.1.0 — four cited Worker test files were deleted.**
> `middleware.test.ts`, `middleware/auth.test.ts`, `middleware/require-role.test.ts` and
> `utils/auth.test.ts` were removed with the undeployed `index.ts` entry point (2.5 Finding 22).
> 246 rows across the four parts cited at least one of them. Two rules were applied.
> **215 rows** named a deleted file only inside a `none found (searched: ...)` list; those rows
> already recorded no equivalent, so only the search target was restated against live code
> (auth → `workers/src/index-minimal.ts` `authenticateApiRequest`:830; roles →
> `workers/src/constants/roles.test.ts`; rate limiting →
> `workers/src/utils/minimal-rate-limit.test.ts`) and **no decision changed**.
> **47 rows** cited a deleted file as a positive equivalent. Of those, 16 were repointed to live
> coverage that genuinely holds — role normalization to `constants/roles.test.ts`, and the
> admin-only gates to `minimal-api-routes.test.ts`, which task 3.1.0 extended to cover
> `handleClearSupplierPolicy` and `handleBulkAttachPolicy`. **31 were REOPENED**: their
> equivalence rested on tests for modules with no production importer, so the claim did not
> survive the deletion. They are marked `REOPENED by task 3.1.0` and carry their superseded
> decision inline. Three groups, none of them a re-pointing exercise:
> **22 auth/JWT rows** (the live Clerk path has no unit coverage for signature, expiry, tenant
> claim or subscription status, and `__tests__/auth-integration.test.ts` asserts
> `const expected = true` in 14 of its 17 cases);
> **2 role rows** for `requireOrgRole(admin, manager)`, which is live Express middleware on the
> user, store-area and organization-invite routes with no admin+manager equivalent anywhere in
> the Worker; and **3 rate-limit rows**, the limiter's reject path being untested in live code.
> These 25 need a decision from the owner; they are not settled.
>
> **Corrected during the same task.** A first pass recorded four further role rows
> (`requirePermission('upload_files')` ×3, `requireMinRole(manager)` ×1) as a live
> authorization gap, reading the Worker's absent upload role check as a regression against
> Express. That was half-verified and wrong: `backend/src/routes/upload.routes.ts` gates uploads
> with `authenticateToken` + `checkUsageLimit` + rate limiters and no role middleware, and
> `requirePermission` (`backend/src/middleware/requireOrgRole.ts:75`) and `requireMinRole`
> (`:110`) occur nowhere outside their own JSDoc examples at `:73` and `:107`. The permission
> model was built and tested on both sides and wired to nothing, so those four rows are
> `retire` — there is no behaviour to port. Upload authorization, if wanted, is net-new product
> work on a fresh decision, not a migration row.

**Task 2.2** — `retire-express-unify-on-postgres`

**Scope:** `backend/src/tests/unit/` — the 50 files alphabetically from `saas-metrics.service`
through `xlsx.upload` (initials `s`–`x`), completing the `unit/` directory. Part 1 covered `a`–`i`,
Part 2 covered `j`–`r`. The non-unit suites (`integration/`, `performance/`, `contract/`,
`security/`, `regression/`) are Part 4. A file in scope with no section is a defect; a section for
a file outside this list is also a defect.

**Status:** Every row carries a `PROPOSED:` prefix. This is an inventory, not a decision
record. The Express suite keeps running on SQLite until deletion; this manifest records what
its coverage maps onto in the Worker suite.

**Columns:** `Behaviour | Test file:line | Named gate | Existing Worker equivalent? | Target | Evidence | Decision`

**Named gates:** `tenant-isolation`, `penetration`, `concurrency`, `feature-limits`,
`webhook-security`, `scheduled-job-idempotency`, `authorization-precedence`, `negative/error`,
or `—`.

**Targets:** `worker-equivalent-exists` | `worker-shaped-rewrite` | `retire`.

**Worker test inventory consulted:** `workers/src/**/*.test.ts` (44 files, enumerated in full
in the task brief before writing rows; re-verified byte-identical to the Part 2 inventory at the
start of this part). Every negative names the files that were opened.

### saas-metrics.service.test.ts

Unit tests for `SaasMetricsService` with a **mocked analytics repository** (`vi.fn()` stubs on
repository methods — no real database, no supertest). The service is a platform-operator
analytics layer: it records Stripe webhook event metrics, computes trial-to-paid conversion
rate, average revenue per user, and subscription tier distribution. The Worker is the
application API for date-management operations and has no SaaS-platform analytics service; its
dashboard test covers operational stats (expiring items, stock loss) for end users, not
operator-facing SaaS metrics.

| Behaviour | Test file:line | Named gate | Existing Worker equivalent? | Target | Evidence | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Records webhook metrics through the analytics repository (incrementWebhookMetrics called once with event name, success flag, timestamp) | saas-metrics.service.test.ts:4 | — | none found (searched: workers/src/__tests__/usage-limits-integration.test.ts, workers/src/__tests__/subscription-validation.test.ts, workers/src/database.dashboard.pglite.node.test.ts, workers/src/clerk/webhook-signature.test.ts) — usage-limits-integration.test.ts:15-54 asserts tier SKU/seat/storage limit constants, not webhook event metrics; subscription-validation.test.ts:17-33 asserts subscription status access control, not webhook analytics; database.dashboard.pglite.node.test.ts:79-108 asserts operational dashboard counts (expiring/expired items), not webhook metrics; clerk/webhook-signature.test.ts verifies Clerk webhook signature validity, not Stripe webhook event analytics recording | retire | Webhook metrics recording is a platform-operator analytics behaviour backed by a Prisma analytics repository with no Worker analogue; the Worker handles Clerk webhooks for auth sync, not Stripe webhook event analytics | PROPOSED: retire — SaaS webhook-metrics analytics is Express/Prisma-specific operator machinery with no Worker analogue |
| Calculates trial conversion rate from analytics repository trials (50% when 1 of 2 ended trials converted) | saas-metrics.service.test.ts:20 | — | none found (searched: workers/src/__tests__/usage-limits-integration.test.ts, workers/src/__tests__/subscription-validation.test.ts, workers/src/database.dashboard.pglite.node.test.ts) — subscription-validation.test.ts:125-141 asserts that a trial subscription is created with status 'trial' and a 14-day end date, but does not compute trial-to-paid conversion rate; usage-limits-integration.test.ts covers tier limit constants, not conversion analytics; database.dashboard.pglite.node.test.ts covers operational item counts, not subscription conversion | retire | Trial conversion rate is a platform-operator SaaS metric computed from a Prisma analytics repository; the Worker has no SaaS analytics service and does not compute conversion rates | PROPOSED: retire — trial conversion analytics is Express/Prisma-specific operator machinery with no Worker analogue |
| Calculates average revenue per user from repository revenue inputs (premium + professional over user count) | saas-metrics.service.test.ts:38 | — | none found (searched: workers/src/__tests__/usage-limits-integration.test.ts, workers/src/__tests__/subscription-validation.test.ts, workers/src/database.dashboard.pglite.node.test.ts) — usage-limits-integration.test.ts:26-41 asserts SKU limit values per tier, not revenue-per-user; subscription-validation.test.ts:191-207 asserts tier_level determines feature access, not ARPU; database.dashboard.pglite.node.test.ts:161-171 sums stock-loss financial values, not subscription revenue | retire | ARPU is a platform-operator SaaS financial metric computed from a Prisma analytics repository; the Worker has no revenue analytics service | PROPOSED: retire — ARPU analytics is Express/Prisma-specific operator machinery with no Worker analogue |
| Gets tier distribution from the analytics repository (maps tier levels to counts) | saas-metrics.service.test.ts:55 | feature-limits | none found (searched: workers/src/__tests__/usage-limits-integration.test.ts, workers/src/__tests__/subscription-validation.test.ts, workers/src/database.dashboard.pglite.node.test.ts) — usage-limits-integration.test.ts:15-54 asserts per-tier limit constants (max_skus, max_users, max_storage), not a distribution count across orgs; subscription-validation.test.ts:191-207 asserts tier_level determines features, not tier distribution counts; database.dashboard.pglite.node.test.ts covers item counts, not subscription tier distribution | retire | Tier distribution is a platform-operator SaaS reporting metric backed by a Prisma analytics repository; the Worker enforces tier limits (feature-limits gate) but does not report on tier distribution across orgs | PROPOSED: retire — tier distribution reporting is Express/Prisma-specific operator analytics; feature-limits gate preserved by Worker usage-limits tests |

### scheduler.service.test.ts

Unit tests for `SchedulerService` with **mocked `node-cron`** (`vi.mock('node-cron')`), mocked
job modules (stripe-sync, trialExpiration, dunning, credit-claim), a mocked `getDb` returning
`prepare()` stubs, and mocked `DatabaseBackupService` / `InventoryService`. No real database,
no supertest — this is a pure wiring test for the Express `node-cron` scheduler that registers
two cron schedules (markdown update at 0 2 * * *, backup at 0 1 * * *) and starts four
background jobs. The Worker has no `node-cron` scheduler; it uses Cloudflare Queue/Scheduled
handlers. The scheduled-job-idempotency gate is preserved by the job-lock rows in Part 2
(worker-shaped-rewrite), not by these cron-registration tests.

| Behaviour | Test file:line | Named gate | Existing Worker equivalent? | Target | Evidence | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

### secure.pin.policies.test.ts

Unit tests for `AuthService` PIN validation and hashing — instantiates `AuthService` directly
(no mocks, no database, no supertest). Tests PIN length/character/predictable-pattern policies
and bcrypt-style hash/verify round-trips. The Worker uses Clerk JWT-based authentication
(verified in `utils/auth.test.ts` and `__tests__/auth-integration.test.ts`); it has no PIN-based
auth, no `validatePin` / `hashPin` / `verifyPin` methods, and no PIN policy enforcement.

| Behaviour | Test file:line | Named gate | Existing Worker equivalent? | Target | Evidence | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| Rejects PINs that are too long (more than 6 digits) | secure.pin.policies.test.ts:31 | negative/error | none found (searched: workers/src/index-minimal.ts, workers/src/__tests__/auth-integration.test.ts) — utils/auth.test.ts:108-117 tests malformed JWT rejection, not long-PIN rejection; auth-integration.test.ts:122-134 tests malformed header rejection, not PIN length validation; no PIN length check exists in the Worker | retire | PIN length validation is Express-specific auth machinery; the Worker has no PIN input surface to validate | PROPOSED: retire — long-PIN rejection is Express-specific auth machinery with no Worker analogue |

**Section removed in task 3.4 (2026-10-02): seed-master-catalogue-cli.test.ts.** The four rows
that stood here proposed `retire` on the grounds that the Worker has no CLI tooling. Task 3.4
rebuilt the seeder as a root operator CLI instead (issue #393), and the Express runner and this
test file were deleted with it, so there is no backend file left for a section to describe. All
four behaviours are rewritten, not retired, in `src/operations/seed-master-catalogue-cli.test.ts`:
argument parsing, argument rejection, the production sample-workbook guard, and CLI JSON error
serialization. The guard is stricter than the one it replaces: it matches on file name, so a
copy of the sample workbook outside the repository is refused too.

### service-provider-constructor.test.ts

Unit tests for `ServiceProvider` — a tsyringe-style dependency injection container that
constructs and caches services/repositories with a `PrismaClient`. Tests instantiate
`ServiceProvider` directly with config objects and mock Prisma clients (no real database, no
supertest). The Worker has no DI container, no `ServiceProvider`, and no `PrismaClient`; it
creates a database via `createWorkersDatabase(env)` (seen in `health.test.ts:1457-1497`) and
calls methods directly.

calls and that `recordUpload` wraps upload-create + usage-increment in a single
`$transaction`. The Worker uses Neon via tagged-template `sql` (no Prisma, no `$transaction`,
no `organizationUsage.upsert` pattern) and enforces storage limits via tier thresholds
(`usage-limits-integration.test.ts:100-138`), but no Worker test asserts the byte-summing,
increment-on-record, or decrement-on-delete behaviour at the DB level.

### storage-quota.routes.test.ts

Route-level tests for the Express `storage-quota` router using **supertest** against a real
Express app with a **mocked `StorageQuotaService`** (constructor, `getStorageQuota`,

headers injected by a stub middleware — no real Clerk, no real database. Tests cover two
endpoints: `GET /storage-quota/:userId` (quota payload) and `GET
/storage-quota/:userId/can-upload` (pre-upload feasibility check), asserting input validation
(non-numeric userId, invalid tier, missing file size), auth/authorization (401 unauthenticated,

(`minimal-api-routes.test.ts:156`) — an org-scoped usage endpoint, not user-scoped — but no
Worker test asserts its validation, auth, payload, or error behaviour. The Worker has no
`/storage-quota/:userId` or can-upload route and no pre-upload quota check test.

### store-area.model.test.ts

Unit tests for `StoreAreaModel` with a **mocked SQLite database** (`db.get`, `db.all`, `db.run`
are `vi.fn()` stubs — no real database, no supertest). Tests cover `createTable` (SQLite DDL via
`sqlite_master` + schema migration via `pragma_index_list`/`pragma_index_info` + DROP/CREATE
reinsert), and CRUD operations (create, findById, findByName,
findByNameAndSubDepartment, findAll, update, delete) with row-mapping from snake_case DB
columns to camelCase model fields. The Worker uses Neon/Postgres via tagged-template `sql`
(no `db.get`/`db.all`/`db.run`, no `sqlite_master`, no `pragma_index_*`); the `store_areas`
table exists in the Worker's Postgres schema (seeded in
`database.store-walk.pglite.node.test.ts:57-71` and `database.dashboard.pglite.node.test.ts:55-56`)
but the Worker has no store-area CRUD methods or routes — only store-walk tracking routes
(`minimal-api-routes.test.ts:187-197` registers check-cycles, bay-checks, floor-progress).

### store-area.repository.test.ts

Unit tests for `StoreAreaRepository` with a **mocked Prisma client** (`storeArea.findMany/findFirst/create/update` and `$queryRaw`/`$executeRaw`/`$transaction` are `vi.fn()` stubs). No real database, no supertest — pure call-shape verification that the repository issues the right Prisma calls and normalizes optional fields. The first four tests cover basic store-area CRUD (list/find/create/update); the last four cover the store-walk tracking layer (check cycles, bay checks, floor progress) which the Worker re-implements on Neon/Postgres via `database.store-walk.pglite.node.test.ts` (real SQL on pglite).

### store-area.routes.test.ts

Express **supertest** route tests for `store-area.routes` with the `StoreAreaService`, auth middleware, `requireOrgRole`, validation middleware, and rate limiter all **mocked**. Tests exercise the route handlers over a real Express app (`request(app)`) but no real database — service methods are `vi.fn()` stubs. Covers two concerns: the store-walk tracking routes (check cycles, bay checks, floor progress), which the Worker re-exposes under `/api/store-areas/*` and tests in `minimal-api-routes.test.ts` + `database.store-walk.pglite.node.test.ts`; and the store-area CRUD routes (GET/POST/PUT/DELETE `/store-areas`), which the Worker does not expose — only store-walk tracking routes are registered per `minimal-api-routes.test.ts:187-197`.

### store-area-service.test.ts

Two describe blocks. The first (`StoreAreaService - findUnique fix`) uses a **real SQLite
Prisma client** (`new PrismaClient` against `test.db`) to verify cross-tenant store-area
isolation and null-on-missing behaviour end-to-end. The second (`StoreAreaService repository
injection`) uses a **mocked `StoreAreaRepository`** (`vi.fn()` stubs) to assert the service
delegates to the injected repository and passes validation errors through — this is
tsyringe/DI wiring plus call-shape verification, not business logic. The Worker has no DI
container and no `getStoreAreaById` handler, but its `database.store-walk.pglite.node.test.ts`
covers the check-cycle, bay-check, and floor-progress behaviours against real Postgres SQL.

### store-walk-tracking.test.ts

Pure unit tests of the **shared domain logic** in `shared/domain/store-walk-tracking`
(`resolveBayState` and `rollupCoverage`). No Prisma, no database, no Express — these are
deterministic pure functions over `StoreWalkBay` and `BayCheckForCycle` inputs. Because the
logic is shared (not Express-specific), it is not itself retirement candidate; the question is
whether the Worker's store-walk suite exercises the same state-resolution and coverage-rollup
behaviours. The Worker's `database.store-walk.pglite.node.test.ts` asserts the end-to-end
floor-progress output (which embodies these rules) against real Postgres SQL.

### stripe-sync.job.test.ts

Unit tests for `StripeSyncJob` — a **node-cron scheduled job** that reconciles subscription
tiers from the Stripe API into the local DB. Uses a **mocked Stripe client**, mocked
`SubscriptionRepository`, mocked `node-cron` scheduler, and mocked tsyringe DI container
(`getDiContainer().resolve`). The sync reconciliation logic (tier divergence, pagination,
missing records, API errors) is business logic with no Worker equivalent — the Worker has no
Stripe sync job and no test file for one. The cron-start test (line 242) is Express/node-cron
+ DI wiring machinery. `subscription-validation.test.ts` is entirely stubs
(`expect(true).toBe(true)`) covering subscription-status gating concepts, not Stripe sync;
`health.test.ts` covers HTTP route dispatch and health checks with no scheduled-handler
coverage.

### stripe-utils.test.ts

Unit tests for the **singleton Stripe client factory** (`getStripeClient` /
`resetStripeClient` / `isStripeConfigured`) backed by `envConfig.STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`. Uses
`vi.doMock('stripe')` and dynamic `import()` to reset module state between cases — no database,
no HTTP. The singleton + module-level mutable state pattern is Express/Node machinery: Workers
are stateless per-request with no module-level singletons. The config-validation behaviours
(missing key, non-secret key) carry the `negative/error` gate and are marked for Worker-shaped
rewrite so the gate is preserved; the Worker reads subscription tiers from the DB, not the
Stripe API, so it has no Stripe client factory today. `subscription-validation.test.ts` is
stubs covering status gating; `health.test.ts` has no Stripe config coverage.

### subscription.routes.test.ts

Unit tests for seven Express subscription routes — `GET /subscription/current`, `GET /organization/usage`, `GET /subscription/trial-status`, `POST /subscription/convert-trial`, `POST /subscription/create-checkout-session`, `POST /subscription/create-portal-session`, and `POST /subscription/cancel` — using **supertest** against an Express app with **mocked Prisma** (`mockFindUnique`), **mocked Stripe client** (`mockStripeCustomersCreate`, `mockStripeCheckoutSessionCreate`, `mockStripeBillingPortalSessionCreate`), **mocked `SubscriptionService`** (`mockConvertTrialToPaid`, `mockCancelSubscription`), and **mocked URL/price validators**. The tsyringe DI container is wired with real imports but mocked instances. Auth middleware is stubbed to set `req.userId` from the `x-clerk-user-id` header; rate limiters are stubbed to pass through. The Worker registers 2 of the 7 subscription routes (`/api/subscription/current` and `/api/organization/usage`) at `minimal-api-routes.test.ts:152` but does not test their handler behaviour; the remaining 5 endpoints have no Worker route registration or behaviour test. The Worker's `subscription-validation.test.ts`, `usage-limits-integration.test.ts`, `feature-gates-integration.test.ts`, and `org-status-integration.test.ts` are stub tests (`expect(true).toBe(true)` or fixture-value assertions) that do not exercise HTTP route behaviour. `utils/feature-gates.test.ts` tests feature-gate logic (`checkFeatureAccess`, `checkUsageLimit`) but not the subscription endpoints.

### subscription-access.helpers.test.ts

Pure unit tests for `hasActiveStripeAccessWindow` — a helper that inspects a Stripe
subscription object (`items.data[0].current_period_end`, `status`, `cancel_at_period_end`)
to decide whether the org still has access. No database, no supertest, no mocks beyond
hand-built Stripe-shaped fixtures. The behaviour under test — "canceled subscription keeps
access until the billing period ends, then closes it" — is a feature-limits/access-control
gate. The Worker's `subscription-validation.test.ts` and `feature-gates-integration.test.ts`
describe this scenario in comments but every assertion is a stub (`expect(true).toBe(true)`
or `expect(sub.status).toBe('canceled')`), so no Worker test actually exercises access-window
determination logic. The Stripe-specific helper shape (`items.data[0].current_period_end`)
retires with the backend, but the access-window behaviour must be re-tested against the
Worker's subscription model in Phase 3.2.

### subscription-billing.helpers.test.ts

Pure unit tests for three Stripe billing helpers — `mapStripeSubscriptionStatusToLocal`,
`getPriceIdForTier`, and `getConfiguredStripePriceIds` — with hand-built fixtures and
`process.env` manipulation for price-ID configuration. No database, no supertest, no mocks.
The status-mapping helper translates Stripe API statuses to internal `SubscriptionStatus`
enum values; the price-ID helpers resolve env-var-configured Stripe Checkout price IDs per
tier/billing-cycle and enforce a Checkout allowlist. The Worker's `subscription-validation.test.ts`
stubs describe subscription status checking but never test a Stripe→local status mapping
function or any Checkout price-ID logic; no Worker test file in the 44-file inventory covers
Stripe Checkout integration. The status-mapping behaviour is relevant domain logic the Worker
would need if it handles Stripe subscriptions (untested → rewrite); the Checkout price-ID
configuration helpers are Stripe-Checkout-specific machinery with no Worker test analogue
(retire); the "reject non-Checkout tiers" guard is a feature-limits named gate that must be
preserved (untested → rewrite).

### subscription-lifecycle-services.test.ts

Unit tests for three subscription lifecycle services — `SubscriptionAccessService`,
`SubscriptionTrialLifecycleService`, and `SubscriptionBillingLifecycleService` — using
**mocked Prisma and mocked Stripe** (`vi.fn()` stubs on `prisma.*` and `stripe.*`). No real
database, no real Stripe API, no supertest. Tests cover access-check isolation from mutation
workflows, trial-setup orchestration (Stripe customer + Prisma subscriptionTier/usage/trialEvent),
missing-organization error handling, and billing cancellation orchestration (Stripe subscription
update + Prisma subscriptionTier update). The Worker's `subscription-validation.test.ts` stubs
describe trial creation (:125) and cancellation (:50) but never test the orchestration logic;
no Worker test covers lifecycle service coordination between Stripe and the database. The
Prisma-specific call shapes and tsyringe DI construction retire with the backend, but the
lifecycle behaviours (trial setup, cancellation, access-mutation isolation, missing-org error)
are relevant to the Worker and untested.

### subscription-trial.helpers.test.ts

Pure unit test for `buildTrialSubscriptionSetup` — a helper that constructs the data
structures for a trial subscription: `trialEndDate` (UTC midnight after the 14-day trial
period), `subscriptionTierData` (professional tier, trialing status, org/customer IDs),
and `organizationUsageData` (tier limits from `TIER_LIMITS.professional`). No database, no
Stripe, no mocks — just a fixed `startedAt` input and structural assertions on the output.
The Worker's `usage-limits-integration.test.ts` asserts the tier limit *values*
(`:40` max_skus=2000, `:82` max_users=10) that correspond to the same limits used here, but
no Worker test covers the trial-setup helper itself (trial-end-date calculation at UTC
midnight, subscription-tier data assembly, usage-data assembly with tier limits). The
`subscription-validation.test.ts:125` stub describes trial creation but only asserts
`expect(trialSubscription.status).toBe('trial')`.

### supplier-credit.repository.test.ts

Unit tests for `SupplierCreditRepository` with a **mocked Prisma client** (`$queryRaw`,
`brand.findUnique/create/update`, `product.updateMany`, `catalogueCorrection.findFirst/create`,
`catalogueSeedRun.findMany`, `supplier.updateMany/findFirst` are all `vi.fn()` stubs). No real
database, no supertest — this is a pure call-shape and orchestration test. Tests cover catalogue
enrichment (retired filtering, barcode→SKU fallback, ambiguous-SKU unmatched, confirmed-brand
reuse), catalogue provenance pagination, policy review ordering, numbered catalogue review with
filtering/pagination, and policy clearing. The Worker has strong equivalents: `catalogue-import-upsert.node.test.ts`
tests enrichment end-to-end with real SQL (pglite), `database.catalogue-provenance.node.test.ts`
tests provenance pagination, and `database.supplier-policy.conformance.node.test.ts` tests policy
review ordering, numbered review filtering, and policy clearing — all with real SQL and SQLite
conformance checks. The Prisma-specific call shapes retire with the backend, but the behaviours
are covered by Worker tests with real SQL.

### supplier-credit.routes.test.ts

Route-wiring tests for the Express `supplierCreditRouter` and `platformCatalogueCorrectionRouter`
using **supertest** against a real Express app with **mocked controllers** (`vi.hoisted` mocks
that return 204). Tests verify route registration (11 supplier-credit routes, 3 platform routes),
platform-router mounting behind `authenticateToken` (by reading `index.ts` source), and input
validation (non-numeric supplier ID → 400 before controller access, via `it.each` over
PUT/PATCH/DELETE). The Worker's `minimal-api-routes.test.ts:161` registers all supplier-credit
and platform routes in the Worker route table, covering the route-registration behaviours. The
Express `app.use` mounting with `authenticateToken` is Express-specific middleware wiring with
no Worker analogue (the Worker uses a route table with auth middleware applied universally).
The input-validation behaviour (reject non-numeric ID) is handled differently by the Worker
(regex route patterns non-match → 404, not Express 400) and must be re-tested.

### supplier-credit.service.test.ts

Unit tests for `SupplierCreditService` and its controller with a **mocked
`SupplierCreditRepository`** (`vi.fn()` stubs for every repo method; `withTransaction`
runs the callback against `undefined`). No real database, no supertest — this is a pure
service-layer test of authorization precedence (admin vs team_member field-level policy
changes), input validation, partial-merge/timestamp semantics, catalogue-review paging,
bulk brand/product operations, claimable-pool grouping, and write-off disposal. The Worker
has no service layer: `createWorkersDatabase` exposes DB functions called directly from
route handlers, with authorization pushed to coarse path-level RBAC
(`middleware/require-role`). DB-level mechanics are covered by the pglite/better-sqlite3
dual-conformance suites (`database.supplier-policy.conformance`,
`database.credit-claim.conformance`); field-level authz and service-boundary validation are
not. `it.each` cases are expanded one row per parameterized case.

### supplier-credit-brand-review-parity.regression-1.test.ts

Regression test for ISSUE-QA-002/004 (Express returned raw Prisma products instead of Worker
DTOs). Uses a **mocked Prisma client** (`product.findMany` stubbed with `vi.fn`) — no real
database, no supertest. Asserts `SupplierCreditRepository.reviewBrands` maps raw Prisma product
rows into the shared `{productId, sku, barcode, productName, brand}` contract that the
Worker/frontend expect, with cursor pagination (`nextCursor`).

| Behaviour | Test file:line | Named gate | Existing Worker equivalent? | Target | Evidence | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Maps product IDs/names to the shared Worker/frontend response contract (`productId`, `sku`, `barcode`, `productName`, `brand`, `nextCursor`) | supplier-credit-brand-review-parity.regression-1.test.ts:8 | — | workers/src/database.supplier-policy.conformance.node.test.ts:322 — `db.reviewBrands(ORG, options)` is asserted to equal `sqliteCatalogueReview(...)` whose items are `{productId, sku, barcode, productName, brand}` (lines 93-99), the same shared DTO contract; line 325-327 also asserts page-2 cursor pagination | worker-equivalent-exists | The Worker database test asserts the identical brand-review DTO shape and pagination contract against a SQLite reference, so the parity behaviour the regression guards is already covered on the Worker side | PROPOSED: worker-equivalent-exists — Worker `reviewBrands` conformance test asserts the same shared DTO contract and pagination |

### supplier-policy.test.ts

Unit tests for the **shared domain** module `shared/domain/supplier-policy` — pure functions
with no I/O, no Prisma, no supertest. Covers `hasPolicy`, `brandPolicyStatus`, `isPolicyWrite`,
`creditScopeForSupplier`, and `validatePolicyWrite`. Since the Worker imports these same
functions (conformance test at `database.supplier-policy.conformance.node.test.ts:5`), the
question is whether the Worker suite exercises each behaviour, not whether the code is shared.

### supplier-policy-schema.test.ts

Schema/migration parity tests using **real filesystem reads** (`fs.readFileSync`) and an
**in-memory SQLite database** (`better-sqlite3`). No Prisma client, no supertest. Verifies
(1) Prisma schema files contain supplier policy fields, (2) Neon SQL migration files exist
with correct columns and a conditional backfill, and (3) SQLite migration 017 applies
idempotently and backfills only real policies. The Worker uses Neon PostgreSQL (no Prisma, no
SQLite migrations); its runtime schema is verified by the conformance test which inserts and
queries these columns.

| Behaviour | Test file:line | Named gate | Existing Worker equivalent? | Target | Evidence | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `prisma/schema.prisma` contains every supplier policy field (representativeName, representativeEmail, contactPhone, policyUpdatedAt) | supplier-policy-schema.test.ts:19 | — | workers/src/database.supplier-policy.conformance.node.test.ts:212 — `createSupplier(ORG, data)` with `representativeName`, `representativeEmail`, `contactPhone`, `policyUpdatedAt` fields succeeds and the returned supplier is asserted at line 213, implicitly verifying these columns exist in the Neon schema | worker-equivalent-exists | The Prisma schema file check is Express-specific machinery, but the underlying behaviour (the database schema has these fields) is covered by the Worker conformance test which creates and queries suppliers with all four fields | PROPOSED: worker-equivalent-exists — Neon schema has all supplier policy fields, verified by conformance test `createSupplier` with those fields |
| `prisma/production/schema.prisma` contains every supplier policy field | supplier-policy-schema.test.ts:19 | — | workers/src/database.supplier-policy.conformance.node.test.ts:212 — same `createSupplier` call with all policy fields succeeds against the Neon schema, verifying the production schema has these columns; the Worker has a single Neon schema (no separate production Prisma file) | worker-equivalent-exists | The production Prisma schema check is Express-specific; the Worker's single Neon schema is verified at runtime by the conformance test | PROPOSED: worker-equivalent-exists — production Neon schema has all fields, verified by conformance test `createSupplier` |
| Neon migration 0007 ships forward and rollback with conditional backfill (`trim(credit_policy_note) <> ''` → `policy_updated_at = updated_at`) | supplier-policy-schema.test.ts:30 | — | none found (searched: workers/src/database.supplier-policy.conformance.node.test.ts, workers/src/workers-deployment.test.ts, workers/src/health.test.ts) — the conformance test inserts suppliers with explicit `policy_updated_at` values (line 247) and does not test a backfill scenario; workers-deployment.test.ts is a deployment smoke test with no migration-file content checks; no Worker test reads or validates migration SQL files | retire | Migration-file content validation is build-time/migration-time machinery; the Worker's Neon database has migrations applied out-of-band, and the runtime schema is verified by the conformance test; the conditional-backfill SQL check has no Worker runtime analogue | PROPOSED: retire — Neon migration file content validation is migration-time machinery; Worker runtime schema verified by conformance test |
| SQLite migration 017 applies idempotently and backfills only real policies (non-blank note → `policy_updated_at = updated_at`, blank → null) | supplier-policy-schema.test.ts:50 | — | none found (searched: workers/src/database.supplier-policy.conformance.node.test.ts, workers/src/workers-deployment.test.ts) — the conformance test uses Neon PostgreSQL via pglite, not SQLite; no Worker test applies a SQLite migration; the backfill-only-real-policies behaviour is not tested at runtime in the Worker suite | retire | SQLite migration testing is Express-specific machinery (the Worker uses Neon PostgreSQL, not SQLite); the idempotent-apply and conditional-backfill behaviours are migration-time concerns with no Worker runtime analogue | PROPOSED: retire — SQLite migration 017 idempotency/backfill is Express-specific SQLite machinery; Worker uses Neon PostgreSQL |

### trial-abuse-prevention.test.ts

Integration tests for `ClerkWebhookService` trial abuse prevention (task 16A.C.3) using a
**real Prisma client** against SQLite (`file:./test.db`) with mocked `SubscriptionService` and
Sentry. Tests the 90-day trial abuse check: blocking trials for emails that had a trial within
90 days, allowing trials for emails with no recent trial, and detecting duplicate emails during
the `user.created` webhook. The Worker has a Clerk bootstrap handler that creates trials for
new users but does not implement the 90-day email-based abuse check.

| Behaviour | Test file:line | Named gate | Existing Worker equivalent? | Target | Evidence | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Returns ConflictError when a duplicate email is created during user.created webhook | trial-abuse-prevention.test.ts:55 | webhook-security | workers/src/clerk/webhook-handler.node.test.ts:258 - "names the user when the grant re-links an existing address" | worker-equivalent-exists | The Worker does not raise a conflict: a duplicate address arriving under a new Clerk identity re-links the existing user (`upsertClerkUser`), so no second user row and no second trial are created. The outcome the Express test protected is kept through a different mechanism | PROPOSED: worker-equivalent-exists - re-link replaces ConflictError; reviewer to confirm that is the intended abuse control |
| Blocks trial for an email used in a trial within the last 90 days (trial not created, Sentry notified) | trial-abuse-prevention.test.ts:96 | feature-limits | none - the Worker has no email-history check on trial creation. A trial is created only when a Clerk user is new to the Worker (`isNewUser`), and Clerk enforces one account per email | retire | Task 3.2 batch 6. Express-only mechanism: Express checked past trials by email because anyone could register again. The Worker creates an organization and trial only for a user it has not seen (workers/src/clerk/bootstrap-handler.node.test.ts:352 - "creates a new org, trial subscription, and first admin for a brand-new user") and never a second trial for a returning user (workers/src/clerk/bootstrap-handler.node.test.ts:473 - "is created once: a repeat bootstrap adds no organization and no second trial"). Not covered: a person who deletes their Clerk account and signs up again with the same address, or a plus-alias. OPEN, for the reviewer: decide whether an email-history check is wanted before the in-store trial; the sign-up check follow-up from batch 5b is the same question | PROPOSED: retire - email-history trial check not carried: structurally covered by one Clerk account per email |
| Allows trial for an email whose last trial was more than 90 days ago | trial-abuse-prevention.test.ts:155 | feature-limits | none - the Worker has no email-history check on trial creation. A trial is created only when a Clerk user is new to the Worker (`isNewUser`), and Clerk enforces one account per email | retire | Task 3.2 batch 6. Express-only mechanism: Express checked past trials by email because anyone could register again. The Worker creates an organization and trial only for a user it has not seen (workers/src/clerk/bootstrap-handler.node.test.ts:352 - "creates a new org, trial subscription, and first admin for a brand-new user") and never a second trial for a returning user (workers/src/clerk/bootstrap-handler.node.test.ts:473 - "is created once: a repeat bootstrap adds no organization and no second trial"). Not covered: a person who deletes their Clerk account and signs up again with the same address, or a plus-alias. OPEN, for the reviewer: decide whether an email-history check is wanted before the in-store trial; the sign-up check follow-up from batch 5b is the same question | PROPOSED: retire - email-history trial check not carried: structurally covered by one Clerk account per email |
| Allows trial for a completely new email (no prior trial history) | trial-abuse-prevention.test.ts:204 | feature-limits | workers/src/clerk/bootstrap-handler.node.test.ts:234 — asserts `trial[0]?.status === 'trialing'` after `handleOrganizationBootstrap` creates a new org + trial for a brand-new user (`clerk-founder`, `founder@startup.test`), verifying a trial is created for a user with no prior history | worker-equivalent-exists | The bootstrap handler test covers the same outcome (new user → trial created) as the Express test's "allow trial for completely new email"; the Worker does not check email history first, but the behaviour (new email gets a trial) is asserted | PROPOSED: worker-equivalent-exists — bootstrap handler test asserts trial creation for a brand-new user, covering the new-email-gets-trial behaviour |

### unit/upload.controller.test.ts

Unit tests for `UploadController.status` with a **mocked `UploadRepository`**
(`findStatusByFileKey` is a `vi.fn()` stub) and an empty `UploadService`. No real
database, no supertest — this is a pure controller call-shape test verifying the
controller decodes the upload key, fetches status from the repository, maps the
repository row to a response payload (progress %, row counts, columns), and
enforces org-scoped access (403 when the repository row's `organizationId`
differs from the authenticated org). The Worker's equivalent is
`handleUploadStatus` exercised in `health.test.ts` (real `Request`/`Response`
model, mocked Clerk token + mocked R2/DB), which returns status payloads but
uses **user-scoped** key ownership (`userOwnsUploadKey`) rather than org-scoped
denial.

| Behaviour | Test file:line | Named gate | Existing Worker equivalent? | Target | Evidence | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Returns upload status from the repository for the authenticated organization (decodes key, maps row to payload with progress/row counts/columns) | upload.controller.test.ts:25 | — | workers/src/health.test.ts:564 — `handleUploadStatus` returns 200 with `body.status`/`body.progress` for an existing upload key; workers/src/health.test.ts:658 — `handleUploadStatus` returns 200 with `statusBody.importedCount`/`updatedCount`/`skippedCount`/`rowsProcessed`/`rowsTotal`, asserting the status payload shape | worker-equivalent-exists | The status-return behaviour is covered by health.test.ts:564 (status + progress) and :658 (full row-count payload); the Express controller's repository→payload mapping is Express-specific wiring, but the status-payload behaviour is equivalent in the Worker | PROPOSED: worker-equivalent-exists — upload status payload covered by health.test.ts:564 (status/progress) and :658 (row counts); controller repository-mapping is Express-specific |
| Denies status access for another organization's upload (403 + Access denied when repository row organizationId differs) | upload.controller.test.ts:70 | tenant-isolation | workers/src/storage-quota-and-upload-guards.test.ts:371 - "refuses to complete, or report the status of, a key that belongs to another user" | worker-equivalent-exists | Task 3.2 batch 6. The Worker has no service layer for this; the rule is in the route handler, the parser or the SQL. The cited test drives the real handler (stubbed database), the real parser, or real SQL (pglite) and asserts the response or the stored rows. Mutation-verified where this batch added the test: breaking the guarded line fails it. The key path carries the user id, so a key from another organization is another user's key; queued-job status is also filtered by organization_id in the SQL (index-minimal.ts handleUploadStatus) | PROPOSED: worker-equivalent-exists |

### upload.repository.test.ts

Unit tests for `UploadRepository` with a **mocked Prisma client** (`$queryRaw`
and `upload.updateMany` are `vi.fn()` stubs). No real database — this is a pure
call-shape test verifying the repository issues the right Prisma calls to find
upload status by file key and to mark an upload completed/failed with metrics.
The Worker uses raw SQL via `database.sql()` (no Prisma), and exercises the
same upload-status transitions end-to-end in the catalogue-import node test
suite against real Postgres (pglite).

### upload.routes.test.ts

Route-dispatch tests using **supertest** against a real Express app with
**mocked controllers/middleware** (`vi.mock` for auth, `checkUsageLimit`,
`validateRequest`, rate limiters, and `createUploadControllerForRequest`). No
real database — the controllers are stubbed to return `{ route: '<name>' }`.
Tests verify route→controller delegation with org context, the storage usage
guard wiring (3× `checkUsageLimit('storage_bytes')`), and a 10MB file-size
rejection (413) via Express's `LIMIT_FILE_SIZE` error middleware. The Worker
uses a `Request`/`Response` router model (`upload-router.test.ts`) and a
route-exists smoke test (`health.test.ts`), with no mocked-controller
delegation test or Express error middleware.

### unit/upload.service.test.ts

Unit tests for `UploadService` with **mocked `StorageProvider`**, **mocked
`CSVParserService`**, **mocked `StorageQuotaService`**, and **mocked
`UploadRepository`** (all `vi.fn()` stubs). No real database or storage — this
is a pure orchestration test verifying the service selects direct vs presigned
upload strategy by file size, enforces a max-size limit, completes uploads
(exists→download→parse→recordUpload→markCompleted), routes import types
(product-catalog vs expiry-list), rejects cross-org keys, handles direct
uploads, and deletes uploads with quota updates. The Worker splits the
complete-upload flow into a queue + `processCatalogueImportJob` (tested in
`catalogue-import-upsert.node.test.ts` against real Postgres) and uses R2
directly (no `StorageQuotaService`).

### upload-status.test.ts

State-machine tests using a **real `PrismaClient`** against SQLite (creates
org/user/upload rows in `beforeEach`, deletes in `afterEach`). Verifies the
canonical upload lifecycle `pending → uploading → processing →
completed/failed`, storage-quota exclusion of failed uploads, `UploadStatus`
enum validity (no legacy 'complete'), and terminal-state immutability
(documentation-style — creates a terminal upload and asserts its status, no
actual transition attempt). The Worker uses string-literal statuses ('queued',
'processing', 'completed', 'completed_with_errors', 'failed') with no
`UploadStatus` enum, and exercises the same transitions end-to-end in
`catalogue-import-upsert.node.test.ts` against real Postgres (pglite).

### url-validator.test.ts

Pure unit tests for two Express utility functions — `validateRedirectUrl`
(open-redirect prevention: allows relative URLs, rejects empty/malformed/non-http
protocols, allow-lists FRONTEND_URL/CORS_ORIGIN domains, allows localhost in
test, rejects non-allow-listed domains) and `validateStripePriceId` (Stripe
checkout price-ID validation: accepts configured IDs, rejects unconfigured/empty/
wrong-prefix/too-short/too-long IDs). No database, no HTTP — these are pure
function tests using `envConfig`/`process.env` mocks. The Worker has **no
redirect-URL flow** (no success_url/cancel_url/returnTo) and **no Stripe
checkout flow** (no priceId/checkout-session surface), so neither utility has
a Worker analogue. The Worker's `authorized-parties.test.ts` covers
FRONTEND_URL-based origin allow-listing for Clerk parties (a related but
different security surface).

### user.repository.test.ts

Unit tests for `UserRepository` with a **mocked Prisma client** (`prisma.user.findMany` /
`findFirst` / `findUnique` / `create` / `update` are `vi.fn()` stubs). No real database, no
supertest — every test asserts the repository issues the correct Prisma call shape (`where`,
`data`, `include`, `select`) and returns the mocked value. The Worker has no Prisma and no
repository class; it issues raw SQL via Neon. The closest Worker analogue is
`clerk/bootstrap-handler.node.test.ts`, which exercises real SQL (pglite) for user
lookup/creation/linking during organization bootstrap. `utils/auth.test.ts` covers JWT/subscription
auth context, not user-table CRUD.

### user.routes.test.ts

Supertest route tests for the Express `/users` router with a **mocked `UserService`** (all service
methods are `vi.fn()` stubs). Auth middleware is stubbed to set `req.organizationId` from the
`x-org-id` header; `requireOrgRole` is stubbed to always pass. Tests cover the full CRUD surface
(GET list, GET by id, POST create, PUT update, DELETE) plus org-scoped access control (403
cross-org), input validation (400 invalid id, missing pin/role), auth context (401 missing org),
and error forwarding (500 on service failure). The Worker has **no user CRUD endpoints** —
`minimal-api-routes.test.ts` registers routes for products, reports, supplier-credits,
store-areas, markdown-config, and subscription, but no `/api/users` routes. The Worker handles
users only via `clerk/bootstrap-handler` (create/link on org bootstrap) and auth context lookup
(`utils/auth.test.ts`). The Express route wiring, supertest dispatch, mocked-service DI, and
Express error middleware are Express-specific machinery with no Worker analogue.

### user.service.test.ts

Service-layer unit tests for `UserService` with a **mocked Prisma client** and **mocked
`AuthService`** (all Prisma methods and `validatePin`/`hashPin`/`verifyPin` are `vi.fn()` stubs).
Tests cover createUser (PIN validation, duplicate check, Prisma create), getUsers (list by org),
getUserById (findFirst scoped to org), getUserByPin (disabled — always returns undefined since
Clerk auth replaced PIN auth), updateUser (Prisma update with P2025 not-found handling), deleteUser
(Prisma delete with P2025 not-found handling), and a repository-injection test verifying the
tsyringe DI pattern. The Worker has no `UserService` class, no Prisma, no `AuthService`, no PIN
auth, and no DI container — it uses Clerk JWT auth and raw SQL via Neon. The service-layer
orchestration, PIN validation/hashing, Prisma call shapes, P2025 error handling, and tsyringe DI
wiring are all Express-specific machinery with no Worker analogue. User creation behaviour is
partially covered by `bootstrap-handler.node.test.ts` but via a different mechanism (org bootstrap
with clerk_user_id, not standalone create with PIN).

### validate-tier-flags.test.ts

Unit tests for the `validate-tier-flags` utility — a **Prisma/SQLite bootstrap validator** that checks the `TierFeatureFlag` database table contains all required feature flags with correct limit values, and can seed missing rows. Uses a **real Prisma client** against SQLite (seeds and deletes rows in `beforeEach`/`afterEach`). The Worker has no `TierFeatureFlag` table — tier limits are hardcoded as `TIER_LIMITS` in `workers/src/utils/auth.ts` and tested via `feature-gates.test.ts` — so the DB validation and seeding behaviours have no Worker analogue, while the constant-value assertions (correct limits per tier, required features/tiers lists) map onto the Worker's hardcoded-constant tests.

### validateRequest.test.ts

Unit tests for the Express `validateRequest` Zod validation middleware factory — `validateRequest`, `validateBody`, `validateParams`, `validateQuery`. Uses **mocked Express `Request`/`Response`/`NextFunction`** objects (no supertest, no real HTTP) to verify that valid requests call `next()` with no error, invalid requests call `next(new ValidationError(...))` with structured field-level errors, and edge cases (nested objects, arrays, empty body, non-Zod errors) are handled. The Worker has no equivalent Zod validation middleware — it validates inline at the route handler level (e.g., `minimal-api-routes.test.ts:399-422` rejects non-numeric percentages with 400) — so the middleware mechanism retires but the validation *behaviours* (reject invalid input, report field errors, standardized error format) map to Worker-shaped rewrites where no handler-level equivalent exists.

| Behaviour | Test file:line | Named gate | Existing Worker equivalent? | Target | Evidence | Decision |

### validation.middleware.test.ts

Unit tests for Express body-validation middleware (`validateProductInput`,
`validateInventoryItemInput`, `validateUserInput`, `validateStoreAreaInput`) using **mocked
`Request`/`Response`/`NextFunction`** — no real database, no supertest. Each validator either calls
`next()` on valid input or returns a 400 JSON error with a field-specific message. The Worker has
no Express-style validation middleware; input validation is done inline in route handlers
(`index-minimal.ts`) and in CSV parsers (`catalogue-parser`, `expiry-parser`), tested via
`minimal-api-routes.test.ts` and the upload parser suites.

| Behaviour | Test file:line | Named gate | Existing Worker equivalent? | Target | Evidence | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Calls next() for valid product data (barcode, sku, name, cost_price) | validation.middleware.test.ts:23 | — | workers/src/upload/catalogue-parser.test.ts:5-21 — accepts a product catalogue row with sku/name/barcode/costPrice and asserts the parsed shape | worker-equivalent-exists | The Worker validates product fields during CSV catalogue import, not in a request-body middleware; the field set and acceptance behaviour overlap (barcode, sku, name, cost) | PROPOSED: worker-equivalent-exists — product field validation covered by catalogue-parser acceptance test |
| Returns 400 for invalid barcode format (too short) | validation.middleware.test.ts:39 | negative/error | none - the Worker accepts a short barcode on create and edit, by decision | retire | Task 3.2 batch 6. Express-only mechanism: Express refused a barcode under 8 characters. The product create and update routes use the permissive mode of the shared validator, so short legacy barcodes and PLU codes are accepted; pinned at workers/src/minimal-api-routes.test.ts:1660 - "accepts a short barcode, which Express would have refused" and workers/src/minimal-api-routes.test.ts:1104 - "still allows a short legacy barcode to be edited". Reviewer: confirm | PROPOSED: retire - barcode-format rule relaxed by decision |
| Returns 400 for invalid SKU format (too long) | validation.middleware.test.ts:56 | negative/error | none - the Worker product routes do not apply the strict SKU length rule | retire | Task 3.2 batch 6. Express-only mechanism: strict mode (`validateProductWrite` with the strict flag) still refuses an over-long SKU (workers/src/product-validation.test.ts:175 - "refuses over-long or non-alphanumeric skus"), but the product create and update routes use the permissive mode. ### webhook-event-dispatcher.test.ts

Unit tests for `dispatchStripeWebhookEvent` — a pure dispatcher that routes a `Stripe.Event` to
the correct handler callback based on `event.type`. Uses **mocked handler functions** (`vi.fn()`)
with no real Stripe, no HTTP, no database. The Worker has Clerk webhook signature verification
(`clerk/webhook-signature.test.ts`) but no Stripe webhook event-dispatch test; the Worker's
subscription tests cover status-based access control, not event routing.

| Behaviour | Test file:line | Named gate | Existing Worker equivalent? | Target | Evidence | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Routes `customer.subscription.created` events to the subscription-created handler (and does not call unhandled) | webhook-event-dispatcher.test.ts:5 | webhook-security | workers/src/stripe/webhook-handler.node.test.ts:283 - "writes the subscription a created event describes" | worker-equivalent-exists | Dispatch is a switch inside the handler rather than a separate dispatcher; the handled event types are asserted end to end | PROPOSED: worker-equivalent-exists |
| Routes unknown event types to the unhandled callback | webhook-event-dispatcher.test.ts:31 | webhook-security | workers/src/stripe/webhook-handler.node.test.ts:814 - "acknowledges an event type it does not act on, without touching subscriptions" | worker-equivalent-exists | Unhandled types are acknowledged and logged | PROPOSED: worker-equivalent-exists |

### webhook-monitoring.test.ts

Unit tests for `HourlyWebhookCheckJob` — a scheduled monitoring job that queries
`SaasMetricsService` (mocked) for webhook health metrics and raises Sentry alerts (mocked
`@sentry/node`) when thresholds are breached. No real database, no real Sentry — this is a pure
threshold/alert logic test. The Worker has no scheduled monitoring job, no Sentry alerting test,
and no webhook-metrics service; Cloudflare Queue ack/retry handles webhook reliability instead.

| Behaviour | Test file:line | Named gate | Existing Worker equivalent? | Target | Evidence | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Captures a Sentry alert when daily webhook error count exceeds 1 | webhook-monitoring.test.ts:33 | webhook-security | workers/src/scheduled/scheduled.pglite.node.test.ts:409 - "alerts on daily error count even when the rate is fine" | worker-equivalent-exists | Real `webhook_metrics` rows drive the job; Sentry capture is asserted with the alert fingerprint | PROPOSED: worker-equivalent-exists |
| Does NOT alert when daily webhook error count is 0 | webhook-monitoring.test.ts:47 | webhook-security | workers/src/scheduled/scheduled.pglite.node.test.ts:369 - "stays quiet below every threshold" asserts no Sentry message | worker-equivalent-exists | Quiet path asserted against real SQL | PROPOSED: worker-equivalent-exists |
| Captures a Sentry alert when replay-attack growth rate exceeds threshold (15x baseline) | webhook-monitoring.test.ts:61 | webhook-security | workers/src/scheduled/scheduled.pglite.node.test.ts:456 - the "replay baseline" table (10 / 51 alerts, 0 / 101 alerts) | worker-equivalent-exists | The Worker rule is a ratio above 5x once the baseline is at least 10, else a volume rule above 100; the express 15x example is covered at the boundary | PROPOSED: worker-equivalent-exists |
| Does NOT alert when growth rate is normal (<=5x baseline) | webhook-monitoring.test.ts:75 | webhook-security | workers/src/scheduled/scheduled.pglite.node.test.ts:456 - the "replay baseline" table (10 / 50 stays quiet) | worker-equivalent-exists | Normal growth does not alert | PROPOSED: worker-equivalent-exists |

### webhook.routes.test.ts

**supertest** against a real Express app with **mocked services** (`webhookService`,
`clerkWebhookService`, `ApplicationMonitoringService`, `@sentry/node`). Tests cover signature
header validation, signature verification, duplicate-event idempotency, successful processing, and
error classification (200 for non-recoverable vs. 500 for transient). The Worker has a Clerk

### webhook.service.test.ts

Unit tests for `WebhookService` — the Stripe webhook event processor. Uses a **mocked Prisma
client** (`processedWebhookEvent`, `organization`, `subscriptionTier`, `organizationUsage`,
`auditLog`, `trialEvent` are all `vi.fn()` stubs) plus a mocked `Stripe` instance and a mocked
`EmailService`. No real database, no supertest, no HTTP — this is a pure call-shape test
verifying that each Stripe event type triggers the right Prisma writes, emails, audit logs, and
idempotency guards. The Worker has no Stripe webhook handler test at all; its only webhook test
(`clerk/webhook-signature.test.ts`) verifies Clerk Svix signature verification, a different
provider and concern. The subscription/org/usage/feature-gates Worker suites are spec stubs
(`expect(true).toBe(true)`) that assert status-gating contracts, not Stripe event processing.

### xlsx.upload.test.ts

Unit tests for `ProductService.processCSVUpload` invoked on **real `.xlsx` files written to
disk** via the `xlsx` library, with a **mocked Prisma client** (`product.findUnique`/`create`/
`findMany`/`update`/`upsert` and `organizationUsage.findUnique` are `vi.fn()` stubs). Despite the
name `processCSVUpload`, the fixture builder uses `XLSX.utils.aoa_to_sheet` + `XLSX.writeFile` to
produce `.xlsx` spreadsheets, then reads them back through the service. The Worker upload path
parses **CSV** (not XLSX) via `csv-parser` + `catalogue-parser`, runs the import through a queued
`processCatalogueImportJob` against Postgres (pglite real-SQL in tests), and stores files in R2.
Header-alias mapping, invalid-cost rejection, and the insert/update classification have direct
Worker equivalents; XLSX-specific file handling and the missing-column/unexpected-column error
messages do not.

| Behaviour | Test file:line | Named gate | Existing Worker equivalent? | Target | Evidence | Decision |
